# Data: 4-model panel text 28/28 + vision 48/48 on the 514pp build (76 files;
# 33 stubs purged and re-collected via retry_split + sequential vision).
# Merge: common 29 / unique 864 -> 710 unique findings.
# Disposition: 36 verification batches (6 waves x 6 subagents, DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash
# critical path; 1 batch re-dispatched after a corrupted output). Verdicts:
# 259 CONFIRMED / 283 DEFENSIBLE / 168 FALSE_POSITIVE.

# (5 figure re-renders/new generator, 1 fig_batch2 line, 1 Needspace) + 3
# build-level overlap fixes (Fig 16.1 legend, Table 13-1 column widths,
# Table 15-1 cell). Rebuild 519pp (514->519), all gates green, overlap 0.

| P2 | Chapter 2 — What Actually Happens During Inference (CH2-01) | The text says BF16 trades mantissa bits from 10 to 8 relative to FP16. BF16 has 7 explicit fraction bits (8 bits of significand precision including the implicit leading bit), while FP16 has 10 explicit fraction bits (11 bits of significand precision). The stated 10 → 8 is not the correct bit accounting. | chapter-02.md:164 'trades mantissa bits (10 -> 8)'; binary16 has 10 fraction bits, bfloat16 has 7; correct pair 10 -> 7 (or 11 -> 8 significand). | Replace “trades mantissa bits (10 → 8) for exponent range” with an accurate statement, e.g. “trades fraction bits (10 → 7) for exponent range” or “trades significand precision (11 → 8 bits including the implicit leading bit) for exponent range.” | FIXED | Fixed: ch02:164 -> 'trades mantissa precision for exponent range (10 -> 7 explicit mantissa bits, 11 -> 8 with the implicit leading bit)'. |
| P2 | Chapter 2 — What Actually Happens During Inference (CH2-02) | The canonical hardware is an H100. H100 tensor cores support FP8 but not FP4. | P2 | Chapter 2 — What Actually Happens During Inference (CH2-03) | Prefix reuse is listed as a way to reduce input tokens. Prefix caching/KV reuse does not reduce the number of input tokens in the request; it avoids recomputing K/V for a shared prefix. It can reduce effective prefill compute, but the prompt still contains those tokens and they may still be billed depending on provider policy. | chapter-02.md:133 'fewer input tokens (retrieval quality, prefix reuse)'; vocabulary table (:35) defines prefix caching as work saving on same tokens, not token-count cut. | Rewrite to separate the levers: “fewer input tokens (retrieval quality, context compression) or less prefill work for the same tokens (prefix reuse).” | FIXED | Fixed: ch02:133 -> 'fewer input tokens (retrieval quality, context compression) or less prefill work for the same tokens (prefix reuse)'. |

| P1 | Ch 26 (CH26-01) | The baseline is placed at the host's analytical capacity ceiling: offered load ~2.0 rps against a per-host ceiling of ~2.0 rps, i.e. rho ~ 0.98-1.0. The declared 320 ms compute term (and hence the 450 ms baseline p99) is not consistent with the declared offered load under any standard queueing model. Continuous batching (or chunked prefill) is what keeps the prefill burst from serialising behind the whole batch.' | DEFENDED | Defended at verification: ch4:249 gives mechanism (queueing, admission, p95 TTFT past 2 s); reviewer numbers check but claim qualified. |
